People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well… it’s the kitchen, mostly. Or that’s how it started, anyway. We had this contractor come in, and for almost two years now I’ve been sure he and some people on my block have been working together, quietly, to cheat us. They keep leaving things half-wrong on purpose, and then they act like I’m the one making a fuss.

My husband keeps showing me receipts and saying there are explanations, but receipts don’t tell you what people are really doing, do they? I mean, I’ve lived on that block a long time, I know when people are being funny. It’s been wearing me down.

Not really, no. I mean, at first I tried to give everyone the benefit of the doubt—because mistakes happen, don’t they? My brother-in-law once had a bathroom done and the tile was crooked, and they blamed the supplier for weeks, so I know how these jobs can go.

The contractor, and some people on the block too, they’ve been quietly working together to cheat us and leave my kitchen half-wrong on purpose. My husband keeps showing me receipts like that proves something, but receipts don’t explain all the excuses and the way things keep happening. So no, I can’t say there’s really been a time I didn’t think that, not once it became clear.

I mean, at first I tried to give everyone the benefit of the doubt—because mistakes happen, don’t they? My brother-in-law once had a bathroom done and the tile was crooked, and they blamed the supplier for weeks, so I know how these jobs can go.\n\nBut this has been nearly two years now. After a while you see the pattern. The contractor, and some people on the block too, they’ve been quietly working together to cheat us and leave my kitchen half-wrong on purpose. My husband keeps showing me receipts like that proves something, but receipts don’t explain all the excuses and the way things keep happening.   },

    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Mistaken? I mean… people always say “maybe you’re mistaken” when they don’t want to look too closely. I’ve thought about that, of course. I’m not unreasonable.

But nearly two years is a long time to be “mistaken,” isn’t it? The kitchen still isn’t right, and every time there’s a reason, some excuse, and then my husband pulls out receipts like we’re in a courtroom. My cousin kept every receipt from her roof repair and still got taken advantage of, so paperwork doesn’t mean people are honest.

So no, I don’t really think I’m mistaken. I’m here because I’m sure the contractor and some people on my block have been working together quietly to cheat us and keep that kitchen half-wrong on purpose.

Sad? I don’t know if I’d call it that. I mean, anyone would be upset when their kitchen’s been left like that and people keep acting like it’s nothing. It wears on you.

Hopeless? I’m not sure that’s the word I’d use. I mean, some days it feels like talking to a brick wall, and that can wear a person down. But “hopeless” sounds so final, doesn’t it?

My mother used to say, if a drawer sticks long enough you stop noticing it until someone else opens it and says, “How do you live like this?” That’s sort of how the kitchen feels now—like everyone expects me to just get used to it. So I don’t know. I’m frustrated, I know that much. Hopeless… I’d have to think about what you mean.

Receipts don’t show the way someone pauses when you ask a question, or how suddenly everyone has an excuse. It’s been nearly two years of this. I wasn’t born yesterday.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that things you see or hear on TV, or read in the newspaper, are specifically about you or sending you a message?\n\nPatient: TV? No, not really TV. I don’t sit there thinking the news anchor is talking to me, if that’s what you mean.\n\nIt’s more right here, on my block, with the contractor and the kitchen. Nearly two years of it now—little things left wrong on purpose, people acting like they don’t know what I’m talking about, excuses every time. My husband keeps waving receipts around like that explains everything, but receipts don’t show what people are really up to. So no, not newspapers sending messages… it’s what’s happening in my own house.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't think the contractor and your neighbors were working against you?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, at first I tried to give everyone the benefit of the doubt—because mistakes happen, don’t they? My brother-in-law once had a bathroom done and the tile was crooked, and they blamed the supplier for weeks, so I know how these jobs can go.\n\nBut this has been nearly two years now. After a while you see the pattern. The contractor, and some people on the block too, they’ve been quietly working together to cheat us and leave my kitchen half-wrong on purpose. My husband keeps showing me receipts like that proves something, but receipts don’t explain all the excuses and the way things keep happening. So no, I can’t say there’s really been a time I didn’t think that, not once it became clear.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that you might be mistaken about the contractor and your neighbors working against you?\n\nPatient: Mistaken? I mean… people always say “maybe you’re mistaken” when they don’t want to look too closely. I’ve thought about that, of course. I’m not unreasonable.\n\nBut nearly two years is a long time to be “mistaken,” isn’t it? The kitchen still isn’t right, and every time there’s a reason, some excuse, and then my husband pulls out receipts like we’re in a courtroom. My cousin kept every receipt from her roof repair and still got taken advantage of, so paperwork doesn’t mean people are honest.\n\nSo no, I don’t really think I’m mistaken. I’m here because I’m sure the contractor and some people on my block have been working together quietly to cheat us and keep that kitchen half-wrong on purpose.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed most of the time since you started noticing these issues with the contractor and your neighbors?\n\nPatient: Sad? I don’t know if I’d call it that. I mean, anyone would be upset when their kitchen’s been left like that and people keep acting like it’s nothing. It wears on you.\n\nBut depressed most of the time… I’m not sure. I don’t sit around using those words. My sister used to say she was “down” whenever the weather changed, you know, especially in winter, and then she’d be fine once she got busy with something. So I don’t know exactly what you mean by depressed. I’m bothered, yes. Frustrated. But sad most of the time? I haven’t really thought of it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless about your situation with the contractor and your neighbors?\n\nPatient: Hopeless? I’m not sure that’s the word I’d use. I mean, some days it feels like talking to a brick wall, and that can wear a person down. But “hopeless” sounds so final, doesn’t it?\n\nMy mother used to say, if a drawer sticks long enough you stop noticing it until someone else opens it and says, “How do you live like this?” That’s sort of how the kitchen feels now—like everyone expects me to just get used to it. So I don’t know. I’m frustrated, I know that much. Hopeless… I’d have to think about what you mean.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

Changed? No, not really. I mean, there are days I get tired of talking about it, because then my husband starts with the receipts again, and I know receipts can make things look proper. My cousin had that happen with her roof—everything looked official until it didn’t.

But my mind hasn’t really wavered. For nearly two years now I’ve been sure the contractor and some people on my block have been quietly working together to cheat us and keep my kitchen half-wrong on purpose. The excuses change, the paperwork changes, but the feeling of what they’re doing hasn’t changed for me.

Anxious most of the time… I don’t know. I mean, I get keyed up when I think about it, sure, because who wouldn’t? You hire someone, you expect the work to be done right, and then suddenly everyone’s acting like you’re the unreasonable one.

It’s more what people do, the way things are left, the timing of it. That’s what I pay attention to.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people, the contractor and your neighbors, are specifically targeting you with their actions?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I mean, not like they announce it, of course, nobody’s going to be that foolish. Receipts don’t show the way someone pauses when you ask a question, or how suddenly everyone has an excuse. It’s been nearly two years of this. I wasn’t born yesterday.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that things you see or hear on TV, or read in the newspaper, are specifically about you or sending you a message?\n\nPatient: TV? No, not really TV. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Angry most of the time… I don’t know if I’d put it that way. I get irritated, yes, especially when I look at the kitchen and remember what was promised. But anger is a big word.

My father used to get angry—slam cabinets, bang tools around in the garage, that sort of thing. I’m not like that. I might mutter to myself or tell my husband he’s not seeing it clearly, but then I’ll go fold laundry or make tea and try to move on. Though, you know, even making tea in that kitchen reminds me of the whole thing, so it circles back.

So… angry most of the time? I’m not sure. Frustrated, definitely. Put out. But I’d have to think about whether that’s anger or just being tired of it.

They keep leaving things half-wrong on purpose, and then they act like I’m the one making a fuss.\n\nMy husband keeps showing me receipts and saying there are explanations, but receipts don’t tell you what people are really doing, do they? I mean, I’ve lived on that block a long time, I know when people are being funny. It’s been wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people can't hear, related to these concerns about your neighbors and the contractor?\n\nPatient: Voices? No, I don’t think so. I mean, I hear plenty—cars, people out front, the usual banging around, especially when everyone’s trash cans are out, and Mrs. Hanley’s dog, honestly, that dog has been barking since it was a puppy—but I don’t think you mean that.\n\nI haven’t noticed, like… a voice talking to me that nobody else hears. It’s more what people do, the way things are left, the timing of it. That’s what I pay attention to.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people, the contractor and your neighbors, are specifically targeting you with their actions?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I mean, not like they announce it, of course, nobody’s going to be that foolish. But it feels very clear to me that the contractor and some people on the block have been working together, quietly, to cheat us and keep that kitchen half-wrong on purpose.\n\nAnd my husband says, “Look, here are the receipts,” and I know he thinks paperwork settles everything, but it doesn’t. Receipts don’t show the way someone pauses when you ask a question, or how suddenly everyone has an excuse. It’s been nearly two years of this. I wasn’t born yesterday.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that things you see or hear on TV, or read in the newspaper, are specifically about you or sending you a message?\n\nPatient: TV? No, not really TV. I don’t sit there thinking the news anchor is talking to me, if that’s what you mean.\n\nIt’s more right here, on my block, with the contractor and the kitchen. Nearly two years of it now—little things left wrong on purpose, people acting like they don’t know what I’m talking about, excuses every time. My husband keeps waving receipts around like that explains everything, but receipts don’t show what people are really up to. So no, not newspapers sending messages… it’s what’s happening in my own house.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't think the contractor and your neighbors were working against you?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, at first I tried to give everyone the benefit of the doubt—because mistakes happen, don’t they? My brother-in-law once had a bathroom done and the tile was crooked, and they blamed the supplier for weeks, so I know how these jobs can go.\n\nBut this has been nearly two years now. After a while you see the pattern. The contractor, and some people on the block too, they’ve been quietly working together to cheat us and leave my kitchen half-wrong on purpose. My husband keeps showing me receipts like that proves something, but receipts don’t explain all the excuses and the way things keep happening. So no, I can’t say there’s really been a time I didn’t think that, not once it became clear.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that you might be mistaken about the contractor and your neighbors working against you?\n\nPatient: Mistaken? I mean… people always say “maybe you’re mistaken” when they don’t want to look too closely. I’ve thought about that, of course. I’m not unreasonable.\n\nBut nearly two years is a long time to be “mistaken,” isn’t it? The kitchen still isn’t right, and every time there’s a reason, some excuse, and then my husband pulls out receipts like we’re in a courtroom. My cousin kept every receipt from her roof repair and still got taken advantage of, so paperwork doesn’t mean people are honest.\n\nSo no, I don’t really think I’m mistaken. I’m here because I’m sure the contractor and some people on my block have been working together quietly to cheat us and keep that kitchen half-wrong on purpose.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed most of the time since you started noticing these issues with the contractor and your neighbors?\n\nPatient: Sad? I don’t know if I’d call it that. I mean, anyone would be upset when their kitchen’s been left like that and people keep acting like it’s nothing. It wears on you.\n\nBut depressed most of the time… I’m not sure. I don’t sit around using those words. My sister used to say she was “down” whenever the weather changed, you know, especially in winter, and then she’d be fine once she got busy with something. So I don’t know exactly what you mean by depressed. I’m bothered, yes. Frustrated. But sad most of the time? I haven’t really thought of it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless about your situation with the contractor and your neighbors?\n\nPatient: Hopeless? So I don’t know. I’m frustrated, I know that much. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t really think so, no. I mean, my husband keeps showing me receipts and messages and saying, “See, it’s all accounted for,” but receipts don’t tell you what people are saying behind your back, do they?

Nearly two years this has been going on, and I’m sure the contractor and some of the neighbors have been quietly working together to cheat us, or at least keep the kitchen wrong on purpose. They always have some explanation, some delay, some little excuse. My cousin had a plumber once who kept “forgetting” parts, and later it turned out—well, never mind, that’s a whole other story.

So unless someone actually admitted what they were doing, I don’t see what information would change it. Paperwork doesn’t prove people aren’t in on something.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… it’s the kitchen, mostly. Or that’s how it started, anyway. We had this contractor come in, and for almost two years now I’ve been sure he and some people on my block have been working together, quietly, to cheat us. They keep leaving things half-wrong on purpose, and then they act like I’m the one making a fuss.\n\nMy husband keeps showing me receipts and saying there are explanations, but receipts don’t tell you what people are really doing, do they? I mean, I’ve lived on that block a long time, I know when people are being funny. It’s been wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people can't hear, related to these concerns about your neighbors and the contractor?\n\nPatient: Voices? No, I don’t think so. I mean, I hear plenty—cars, people out front, the usual banging around, especially when everyone’s trash cans are out, and Mrs. Hanley’s dog, honestly, that dog has been barking since it was a puppy—but I don’t think you mean that.\n\nI haven’t noticed, like… a voice talking to me that nobody else hears. It’s more what people do, the way things are left, the timing of it. That’s what I pay attention to.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people, the contractor and your neighbors, are specifically targeting you with their actions?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I mean, not like they announce it, of course, nobody’s going to be that foolish. But it feels very clear to me that the contractor and some people on the block have been working together, quietly, to cheat us and keep that kitchen half-wrong on purpose.\n\nAnd my husband says, “Look, here are the receipts,” and I know he thinks paperwork settles everything, but it doesn’t. Receipts don’t show the way someone pauses when you ask a question, or how suddenly everyone has an excuse. It’s been nearly two years of this. I wasn’t born yesterday.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that things you see or hear on TV, or read in the newspaper, are specifically about you or sending you a message?\n\nPatient: TV? No, not really TV. I don’t sit there thinking the news anchor is talking to me, if that’s what you mean.\n\nIt’s more right here, on my block, with the contractor and the kitchen. Nearly two years of it now—little things left wrong on purpose, people acting like they don’t know what I’m talking about, excuses every time. My husband keeps waving receipts around like that explains everything, but receipts don’t show what people are really up to. So no, not newspapers sending messages… it’s what’s happening in my own house.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't think the contractor and your neighbors were working against you?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, at first I tried to give everyone the benefit of the doubt—because mistakes happen, don’t they? My brother-in-law once had a bathroom done and the tile was crooked, and they blamed the supplier for weeks, so I know how these jobs can go.\n\nBut this has been nearly two years now. After a while you see the pattern. The contractor, and some people on the block too, they’ve been quietly working together to cheat us and leave my kitchen half-wrong on purpose. My husband keeps showing me receipts like that proves something, but receipts don’t explain all the excuses and the way things keep happening. So no, I can’t say there’s really been a time I didn’t think that, not once it became clear.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that you might be mistaken about the contractor and your neighbors working against you?\n\nPatient: Mistaken? I mean… people always say “maybe you’re mistaken” when they don’t want to look too closely. I’ve thought about that, of course. I’m not unreasonable.\n\nBut nearly two years is a long time to be “mistaken,” isn’t it? The kitchen still isn’t right, and every time there’s a reason, some excuse, and then my husband pulls out receipts like we’re in a courtroom. My cousin kept every receipt from her roof repair and still got taken advantage of, so paperwork doesn’t mean people are honest.\n\nSo no, I don’t really think I’m mistaken. I’m here because I’m sure the contractor and some people on my block have been working together quietly to cheat us and keep that kitchen half-wrong on purpose.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed most of the time since you started noticing these issues with the contractor and your neighbors?\n\nPatient: Sad? I don’t know if I’d call it that. I mean, anyone would be upset when their kitchen’s been left like that and people keep acting like it’s nothing. It wears on you.\n\nBut depressed most of the time… I’m not sure. I don’t sit around using those words. My sister used to say she was “down” whenever the weather changed, you know, especially in winter, and then she’d be fine once she got busy with something. So I don’t know exactly what you mean by depressed. I’m bothered, yes. Frustrated. But sad most of the time? I haven’t really thought of it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless about your situation with the contractor and your neighbors?\n\nPatient: Hopeless? I’m not sure that’s the word I’d use. I mean, some days it feels like talking to a brick wall, and that can wear a person down. But “hopeless” sounds so final, doesn’t it?\n\nMy mother used to say, if a drawer sticks long enough you stop noticing it until someone else opens it and says, “How do you live like this?” That’s sort of how the kitchen feels now—like everyone expects me to just get used to it. So I don’t know. I’m frustrated, I know that much. Hopeless… I’d have to think about what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about the contractor and your neighbors working against you ever changed or wavered over the nearly two years this has been going on?\n\nPatient: Changed? No, not really. I mean, there are days I get tired of talking about it, because then my husband starts with the receipts again, and I know receipts can make things look proper. My cousin had that happen with her roof—everything looked official until it didn’t.\n\nBut my mind hasn’t really wavered. For nearly two years now I’ve been sure the contractor and some people on my block have been quietly working together to cheat us and keep my kitchen half-wrong on purpose. The excuses change, the paperwork changes, but the feeling of what they’re doing hasn’t changed for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious most of the time since you started noticing these issues with the contractor and your neighbors?\n\nPatient: Anxious most of the time… I don’t know. I mean, I get keyed up when I think about it, sure, because who wouldn’t? You hire someone, you expect the work to be done right, and then suddenly everyone’s acting like you’re the unreasonable one.\n\nBut “most of the time” is hard to say. My aunt used to say she had nerves, you know, she’d wring a dish towel until it was practically twisted in half when the phone rang. I’m not like that exactly. I don’t sit there naming it anxiety. I’m alert, maybe. On edge when something reminds me of it. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Lost interest? No, I don’t think so. I mean, the kitchen thing gets in the way mentally sometimes, because it’s right there in the house, and you can’t exactly avoid your own kitchen.

But I still go to church gatherings. I still go to book club, even if half the time someone brings up renovations and then I have to bite my tongue because people think they know better. And I keep up with the house, laundry, meals, seeing family, making plans the way I usually do. My daughter likes when we all get together on Sundays, and I still do that.

So no, I wouldn’t say I’ve stopped enjoying things. It’s more that this remodel business is sitting off to the side of everything, like a radio left on in another room.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… it’s the kitchen, mostly. Or that’s how it started, anyway. We had this contractor come in, and for almost two years now I’ve been sure he and some people on my block have been working together, quietly, to cheat us. They keep leaving things half-wrong on purpose, and then they act like I’m the one making a fuss.\n\nMy husband keeps showing me receipts and saying there are explanations, but receipts don’t tell you what people are really doing, do they? I mean, I’ve lived on that block a long time, I know when people are being funny. It’s been wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people can't hear, related to these concerns about your neighbors and the contractor?\n\nPatient: Voices? No, I don’t think so. I mean, I hear plenty—cars, people out front, the usual banging around, especially when everyone’s trash cans are out, and Mrs. Hanley’s dog, honestly, that dog has been barking since it was a puppy—but I don’t think you mean that.\n\nI haven’t noticed, like… a voice talking to me that nobody else hears. It’s more what people do, the way things are left, the timing of it. That’s what I pay attention to.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people, the contractor and your neighbors, are specifically targeting you with their actions?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I mean, not like they announce it, of course, nobody’s going to be that foolish. But it feels very clear to me that the contractor and some people on the block have been working together, quietly, to cheat us and keep that kitchen half-wrong on purpose.\n\nAnd my husband says, “Look, here are the receipts,” and I know he thinks paperwork settles everything, but it doesn’t. Receipts don’t show the way someone pauses when you ask a question, or how suddenly everyone has an excuse. It’s been nearly two years of this. I wasn’t born yesterday.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that things you see or hear on TV, or read in the newspaper, are specifically about you or sending you a message?\n\nPatient: TV? No, not really TV. I don’t sit there thinking the news anchor is talking to me, if that’s what you mean.\n\nIt’s more right here, on my block, with the contractor and the kitchen. Nearly two years of it now—little things left wrong on purpose, people acting like they don’t know what I’m talking about, excuses every time. My husband keeps waving receipts around like that explains everything, but receipts don’t show what people are really up to. So no, not newspapers sending messages… it’s what’s happening in my own house.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't think the contractor and your neighbors were working against you?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, at first I tried to give everyone the benefit of the doubt—because mistakes happen, don’t they? My brother-in-law once had a bathroom done and the tile was crooked, and they blamed the supplier for weeks, so I know how these jobs can go.\n\nBut this has been nearly two years now. After a while you see the pattern. The contractor, and some people on the block too, they’ve been quietly working together to cheat us and leave my kitchen half-wrong on purpose. My husband keeps showing me receipts like that proves something, but receipts don’t explain all the excuses and the way things keep happening. So no, I can’t say there’s really been a time I didn’t think that, not once it became clear.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that you might be mistaken about the contractor and your neighbors working against you?\n\nPatient: Mistaken? I mean… people always say “maybe you’re mistaken” when they don’t want to look too closely. I’ve thought about that, of course. I’m not unreasonable.\n\nBut nearly two years is a long time to be “mistaken,” isn’t it? The kitchen still isn’t right, and every time there’s a reason, some excuse, and then my husband pulls out receipts like we’re in a courtroom. My cousin kept every receipt from her roof repair and still got taken advantage of, so paperwork doesn’t mean people are honest.\n\nSo no, I don’t really think I’m mistaken. I’m here because I’m sure the contractor and some people on my block have been working together quietly to cheat us and keep that kitchen half-wrong on purpose.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed most of the time since you started noticing these issues with the contractor and your neighbors?\n\nPatient: Sad? I don’t know if I’d call it that. I mean, anyone would be upset when their kitchen’s been left like that and people keep acting like it’s nothing. It wears on you.\n\nBut depressed most of the time… I’m not sure. I don’t sit around using those words. My sister used to say she was “down” whenever the weather changed, you know, especially in winter, and then she’d be fine once she got busy with something. So I don’t know exactly what you mean by depressed. I’m bothered, yes. Frustrated. But sad most of the time? I haven’t really thought of it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless about your situation with the contractor and your neighbors?\n\nPatient: Hopeless? I’m not sure that’s the word I’d use. I mean, some days it feels like talking to a brick wall, and that can wear a person down. But “hopeless” sounds so final, doesn’t it?\n\nMy mother used to say, if a drawer sticks long enough you stop noticing it until someone else opens it and says, “How do you live like this?” That’s sort of how the kitchen feels now—like everyone expects me to just get used to it. So I don’t know. I’m frustrated, I know that much. Hopeless… I’d have to think about what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about the contractor and your neighbors working against you ever changed or wavered over the nearly two years this has been going on?\n\nPatient: Changed? No, not really. I mean, there are days I get tired of talking about it, because then my husband starts with the receipts again, and I know receipts can make things look proper. My cousin had that happen with her roof—everything looked official until it didn’t.\n\nBut my mind hasn’t really wavered. For nearly two years now I’ve been sure the contractor and some people on my block have been quietly working together to cheat us and keep my kitchen half-wrong on purpose. The excuses change, the paperwork changes, but the feeling of what they’re doing hasn’t changed for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious most of the time since you started noticing these issues with the contractor and your neighbors?\n\nPatient: Anxious most of the time… I don’t know. I mean, I get keyed up when I think about it, sure, because who wouldn’t? You hire someone, you expect the work to be done right, and then suddenly everyone’s acting like you’re the unreasonable one.\n\nBut “most of the time” is hard to say. My aunt used to say she had nerves, you know, she’d wring a dish towel until it was practically twisted in half when the phone rang. I’m not like that exactly. I don’t sit there naming it anxiety. I’m alert, maybe. On edge when something reminds me of it. But I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling angry most of the time since you started noticing these issues with the contractor and your neighbors?\n\nPatient: Angry most of the time… I don’t know if I’d put it that way. But anger is a big word.\n\nMy father used to get angry—slam cabinets, bang tools around in the garage, that sort of thing. I’m not like that. I might mutter to myself or tell my husband he’s not seeing it clearly, but then I’ll go fold laundry or make tea and try to move on. Though, you know, even making tea in that kitchen reminds me of the whole thing, so it circles back.\n\nSo… angry most of the time? I’m not sure. Frustrated, definitely. Put out. But I’d have to think about whether that’s anger or just being tired of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could happen, or any information you could receive, that would change your mind about the contractor and your neighbors working against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think so, no. I mean, my husband keeps showing me receipts and messages and saying, “See, it’s all accounted for,” but receipts don’t tell you what people are saying behind your back, do they?\n\nNearly two years this has been going on, and I’m sure the contractor and some of the neighbors have been quietly working together to cheat us, or at least keep the kitchen wrong on purpose. So I don’t know exactly what you mean by depressed. I’m bothered, yes. Frustrated. But sad most of the time? I haven’t really thought of it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless about your situation with the contractor and your neighbors?\n\nPatient: Hopeless? I’m not sure that’s the word I’d use. I mean, some days it feels like talking to a brick wall, and that can wear a person down. But “hopeless” sounds so final, doesn’t it?\n\nMy mother used to say, if a drawer sticks long enough you stop noticing it until someone else opens it and says, “How do you live like this?” That’s sort of how the kitchen feels now—like everyone expects me to just get used to it. So I don’t know. I’m frustrated, I know that much. Hopeless… I’d have to think about what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about the contractor and your neighbors working against you ever changed or wavered over the nearly two years this has been going on?\n\nPatient: Changed? No, not really. I mean, there are days I get tired of talking about it, because then my husband starts with the receipts again, and I know receipts can make things look proper. My cousin had that happen with her roof—everything looked official until it didn’t.\n\nBut my mind hasn’t really wavered. For nearly two years now I’ve been sure the contractor and some people on my block have been quietly working together to cheat us and keep my kitchen half-wrong on purpose. The excuses change, the paperwork changes, but the feeling of what they’re doing hasn’t changed for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious most of the time since you started noticing these issues with the contractor and your neighbors?\n\nPatient: Anxious most of the time… I don’t know. I mean, I get keyed up when I think about it, sure, because who wouldn’t? You hire someone, you expect the work to be done right, and then suddenly everyone’s acting like you’re the unreasonable one.\n\nBut “most of the time” is hard to say. My aunt used to say she had nerves, you know, she’d wring a dish towel until it was practically twisted in half when the phone rang. I’m not like that exactly. I don’t sit there naming it anxiety. I’m alert, maybe. On edge when something reminds me of it. But I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling angry most of the time since you started noticing these issues with the contractor and your neighbors?\n\nPatient: Angry most of the time… I don’t know if I’d put it that way. I get irritated, yes, especially when I look at the kitchen and remember what was promised. But anger is a big word.\n\nMy father used to get angry—slam cabinets, bang tools around in the garage, that sort of thing. I’m not like that. I might mutter to myself or tell my husband he’s not seeing it clearly, but then I’ll go fold laundry or make tea and try to move on. Though, you know, even making tea in that kitchen reminds me of the whole thing, so it circles back.\n\nSo… angry most of the time? I’m not sure. Frustrated, definitely. Put out. But I’d have to think about whether that’s anger or just being tired of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could happen, or any information you could receive, that would change your mind about the contractor and your neighbors working against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think so, no. I mean, my husband keeps showing me receipts and messages and saying, “See, it’s all accounted for,” but receipts don’t tell you what people are saying behind your back, do they?\n\nNearly two years this has been going on, and I’m sure the contractor and some of the neighbors have been quietly working together to cheat us, or at least keep the kitchen wrong on purpose. They always have some explanation, some delay, some little excuse. My cousin had a plumber once who kept “forgetting” parts, and later it turned out—well, never mind, that’s a whole other story.\n\nSo unless someone actually admitted what they were doing, I don’t see what information would change it. Paperwork doesn’t prove people aren’t in on something.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities you normally enjoy since this issue with the contractor and your neighbors started?\n\nPatient: Lost interest? No, I don’t think so. I mean, the kitchen thing gets in the way mentally sometimes, because it’s right there in the house, and you can’t exactly avoid your own kitchen.\n\nBut I still go to church gatherings. I still go to book club, even if half the time someone brings up renovations and then I have to bite my tongue because people think they know better. And I keep up with the house, laundry, meals, seeing family, making plans the way I usually do. My daughter likes when we all get together on Sundays, and I still do that.\n\nSo no, I wouldn’t say I’ve stopped enjoying things. It’s more that this remodel business is sitting off to the side of everything, like a radio left on in another room.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yes… I suppose it has made some things harder. I try not to bring it up all the time, because my daughter gets that look, you know, like she’s waiting for me to change the subject.

With the neighbors, I don’t trust certain ones anymore. I used to wave and chat about their gardens or who was putting out Christmas lights too early—there’s one house that starts in October, which I think is ridiculous—but now I keep it shorter. I don’t know who’s friendly and who’s just listening.

He says it’s easier if he talks to them, but of course that makes me feel pushed aside a bit. Still, yes, it’s affected things.

From the dialogue so far and your current candidates, ask the one follow-up question that best separates them.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Current Candidates]\nF22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… it’s the kitchen, mostly. Or that’s how it started, anyway. We had this contractor come in, and for almost two years now I’ve been sure he and some people on my block have been working together, quietly, to cheat us. They keep leaving things half-wrong on purpose, and then they act like I’m the one making a fuss.\n\nMy husband keeps showing me receipts and saying there are explanations, but receipts don’t tell you what people are really doing, do they? It’s more what people do, the way things are left, the timing of it. That’s what I pay attention to.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people, the contractor and your neighbors, are specifically targeting you with their actions?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I mean, not like they announce it, of course, nobody’s going to be that foolish. Receipts don’t show the way someone pauses when you ask a question, or how suddenly everyone has an excuse. It’s been nearly two years of this. I wasn’t born yesterday.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that things you see or hear on TV, or read in the newspaper, are specifically about you or sending you a message?\n\nPatient: TV? No, not really TV. I don’t sit there thinking the news anchor is talking to me, if that’s what you mean.\n\nIt’s more right here, on my block, with the contractor and the kitchen. Nearly two years of it now—little things left wrong on purpose, people acting like they don’t know what I’m talking about, excuses every time. My husband keeps waving receipts around like that explains everything, but receipts don’t show what people are really up to. So no, not newspapers sending messages… it’s what’s happening in my own house.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't think the contractor and your neighbors were working against you?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, at first I tried to give everyone the benefit of the doubt—because mistakes happen, don’t they? My brother-in-law once had a bathroom done and the tile was crooked, and they blamed the supplier for weeks, so I know how these jobs can go.\n\nBut this has been nearly two years now. After a while you see the pattern. The contractor, and some people on the block too, they’ve been quietly working together to cheat us and leave my kitchen half-wrong on purpose. My husband keeps showing me receipts like that proves something, but receipts don’t explain all the excuses and the way things keep happening. So no, I can’t say there’s really been a time I didn’t think that, not once it became clear.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that you might be mistaken about the contractor and your neighbors working against you?\n\nPatient: Mistaken? I mean… people always say “maybe you’re mistaken” when they don’t want to look too closely. I’ve thought about that, of course. I’m not unreasonable.\n\nBut nearly two years is a long time to be “mistaken,” isn’t it? The kitchen still isn’t right, and every time there’s a reason, some excuse, and then my husband pulls out receipts like we’re in a courtroom. My cousin kept every receipt from her roof repair and still got taken advantage of, so paperwork doesn’t mean people are honest.\n\nSo no, I don’t really think I’m mistaken. I’m here because I’m sure the contractor and some people on my block have been working together quietly to cheat us and keep that kitchen half-wrong on purpose.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed most of the time since you started noticing these issues with the contractor and your neighbors?\n\nPatient: Sad? I don’t know if I’d call it that. I mean, anyone would be upset when their kitchen’s been left like that and people keep acting like it’s nothing. It wears on you.\n\nBut depressed most of the time… I’m not sure. I don’t sit around using those words. My sister used to say she was “down” whenever the weather changed, you know, especially in winter, and then she’d be fine once she got busy with something. So I don’t know exactly what you mean by depressed. I’m bothered, yes. Frustrated. But sad most of the time? I haven’t really thought of it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless about your situation with the contractor and your neighbors?\n\nPatient: Hopeless? I’m not sure that’s the word I’d use. I mean, some days it feels like talking to a brick wall, and that can wear a person down. But “hopeless” sounds so final, doesn’t it?\n\nMy mother used to say, if a drawer sticks long enough you stop noticing it until someone else opens it and says, “How do you live like this?” That’s sort of how the kitchen feels now—like everyone expects me to just get used to it. So I don’t know. I’m frustrated, I know that much. Hopeless… I’d have to think about what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about the contractor and your neighbors working against you ever changed or wavered over the nearly two years this has been going on?\n\nPatient: Changed? No, not really. I mean, there are days I get tired of talking about it, because then my husband starts with the receipts again, and I know receipts can make things look proper. My cousin had that happen with her roof—everything looked official until it didn’t.\n\nBut my mind hasn’t really wavered. For nearly two years now I’ve been sure the contractor and some people on my block have been quietly working together to cheat us and keep my kitchen half-wrong on purpose. The excuses change, the paperwork changes, but the feeling of what they’re doing hasn’t changed for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious most of the time since you started noticing these issues with the contractor and your neighbors?\n\nPatient: Anxious most of the time… I don’t know. I mean, I get keyed up when I think about it, sure, because who wouldn’t? You hire someone, you expect the work to be done right, and then suddenly everyone’s acting like you’re the unreasonable one.\n\nBut “most of the time” is hard to say. My aunt used to say she had nerves, you know, she’d wring a dish towel until it was practically twisted in half when the phone rang. I’m not like that exactly. I don’t sit there naming it anxiety. I’m alert, maybe. On edge when something reminds me of it. But I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling angry most of the time since you started noticing these issues with the contractor and your neighbors?\n\nPatient: Angry most of the time… I don’t know if I’d put it that way. I get irritated, yes, especially when I look at the kitchen and remember what was promised. But anger is a big word.\n\nMy father used to get angry—slam cabinets, bang tools around in the garage, that sort of thing. I’m not like that. I might mutter to myself or tell my husband he’s not seeing it clearly, but then I’ll go fold laundry or make tea and try to move on. Though, you know, even making tea in that kitchen reminds me of the whole thing, so it circles back.\n\nSo… angry most of the time? I’m not sure. Frustrated, definitely. Put out. But I’d have to think about whether that’s anger or just being tired of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could happen, or any information you could receive, that would change your mind about the contractor and your neighbors working against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think so, no. I mean, my husband keeps showing me receipts and messages and saying, “See, it’s all accounted for,” but receipts don’t tell you what people are saying behind your back, do they?\n\nNearly two years this has been going on, and I’m sure the contractor and some of the neighbors have been quietly working together to cheat us, or at least keep the kitchen wrong on purpose. They always have some explanation, some delay, some little excuse. My cousin had a plumber once who kept “forgetting” parts, and later it turned out—well, never mind, that’s a whole other story.\n\nSo unless someone actually admitted what they were doing, I don’t see what information would change it. Paperwork doesn’t prove people aren’t in on something.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities you normally enjoy since this issue with the contractor and your neighbors started?\n\nPatient: Lost interest? No, I don’t think so. I mean, the kitchen thing gets in the way mentally sometimes, because it’s right there in the house, and you can’t exactly avoid your own kitchen.\n\nBut I still go to church gatherings. I still go to book club, even if half the time someone brings up renovations and then I have to bite my tongue because people think they know better. And I keep up with the house, laundry, meals, seeing family, making plans the way I usually do. My daughter likes when we all get together on Sundays, and I still do that.\n\nSo no, I wouldn’t say I’ve stopped enjoying things. It’s more that this remodel business is sitting off to the side of everything, like a radio left on in another room.\n\nDoctor: Have you had difficulty interacting with friends or family because of your concerns about the contractor and your neighbors?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose it has made some things harder. I try not to bring it up all the time, because my daughter gets that look, you know, like she’s waiting for me to change the subject.\n\nWith the neighbors, I don’t trust certain ones anymore. I used to wave and chat about their gardens or who was putting out Christmas lights too early—there’s one house that starts in October, which I think is ridiculous—but now I keep it shorter. I don’t know who’s friendly and who’s just listening.\n\nAnd with workers… my husband has had to handle more of the remodeling decisions lately, because I keep questioning what they’re really doing and whether they’re being straight with us. He says it’s easier if he talks to them, but of course that makes me feel pushed aside a bit. Still, yes, it’s affected things.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
If we moved? Well… I’ve thought about that, actually. Part of me thinks it might be quieter, because then I wouldn’t have to look out and wonder which neighbor is talking to who. But the kitchen would still be the kitchen, wouldn’t it? Nearly two years of this, and it’s not just a bad cabinet or a crooked drawer—though one drawer does stick, and my sister had the same problem in her laundry room remodel.

What I mean is, I’m here because I’m sure the contractor and some people on this block have been working together to cheat us, or at least keep things wrong on purpose. So if we moved, maybe the neighbors wouldn’t be right there anymore, but I don’t know that it would undo what they’ve already done. People can still talk. Contractors know other contractors. It doesn’t just disappear because you pack boxes.

So I don’t know. I’m frustrated, I know that much. Hopeless… I’d have to think about what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about the contractor and your neighbors working against you ever changed or wavered over the nearly two years this has been going on?\n\nPatient: Changed? No, not really. I mean, there are days I get tired of talking about it, because then my husband starts with the receipts again, and I know receipts can make things look proper. My cousin had that happen with her roof—everything looked official until it didn’t.\n\nBut my mind hasn’t really wavered. For nearly two years now I’ve been sure the contractor and some people on my block have been quietly working together to cheat us and keep my kitchen half-wrong on purpose. The excuses change, the paperwork changes, but the feeling of what they’re doing hasn’t changed for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious most of the time since you started noticing these issues with the contractor and your neighbors?\n\nPatient: Anxious most of the time… I don’t know. I mean, I get keyed up when I think about it, sure, because who wouldn’t? You hire someone, you expect the work to be done right, and then suddenly everyone’s acting like you’re the unreasonable one.\n\nBut “most of the time” is hard to say. My aunt used to say she had nerves, you know, she’d wring a dish towel until it was practically twisted in half when the phone rang. I’m not like that exactly. I don’t sit there naming it anxiety. I’m alert, maybe. On edge when something reminds me of it. But I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling angry most of the time since you started noticing these issues with the contractor and your neighbors?\n\nPatient: Angry most of the time… I don’t know if I’d put it that way. I get irritated, yes, especially when I look at the kitchen and remember what was promised. But anger is a big word.\n\nMy father used to get angry—slam cabinets, bang tools around in the garage, that sort of thing. I’m not like that. I might mutter to myself or tell my husband he’s not seeing it clearly, but then I’ll go fold laundry or make tea and try to move on. Though, you know, even making tea in that kitchen reminds me of the whole thing, so it circles back.\n\nSo… angry most of the time? I’m not sure. Frustrated, definitely. Put out. But I’d have to think about whether that’s anger or just being tired of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could happen, or any information you could receive, that would change your mind about the contractor and your neighbors working against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think so, no. I mean, my husband keeps showing me receipts and messages and saying, “See, it’s all accounted for,” but receipts don’t tell you what people are saying behind your back, do they?\n\nNearly two years this has been going on, and I’m sure the contractor and some of the neighbors have been quietly working together to cheat us, or at least keep the kitchen wrong on purpose. They always have some explanation, some delay, some little excuse. My cousin had a plumber once who kept “forgetting” parts, and later it turned out—well, never mind, that’s a whole other story.\n\nSo unless someone actually admitted what they were doing, I don’t see what information would change it. Paperwork doesn’t prove people aren’t in on something.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities you normally enjoy since this issue with the contractor and your neighbors started?\n\nPatient: Lost interest? No, I don’t think so. I mean, the kitchen thing gets in the way mentally sometimes, because it’s right there in the house, and you can’t exactly avoid your own kitchen.\n\nBut I still go to church gatherings. I still go to book club, even if half the time someone brings up renovations and then I have to bite my tongue because people think they know better. And I keep up with the house, laundry, meals, seeing family, making plans the way I usually do. My daughter likes when we all get together on Sundays, and I still do that.\n\nSo no, I wouldn’t say I’ve stopped enjoying things. It’s more that this remodel business is sitting off to the side of everything, like a radio left on in another room.\n\nDoctor: Have you had difficulty interacting with friends or family because of your concerns about the contractor and your neighbors?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose it has made some things harder. I try not to bring it up all the time, because my daughter gets that look, you know, like she’s waiting for me to change the subject.\n\nWith the neighbors, I don’t trust certain ones anymore. I used to wave and chat about their gardens or who was putting out Christmas lights too early—there’s one house that starts in October, which I think is ridiculous—but now I keep it shorter. I don’t know who’s friendly and who’s just listening.\n\nAnd with workers… my husband has had to handle more of the remodeling decisions lately, because I keep questioning what they’re really doing and whether they’re being straight with us. He says it’s easier if he talks to them, but of course that makes me feel pushed aside a bit. Still, yes, it’s affected things.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the contractor and your neighbors would still be working against you if you moved to a different house?\n\nPatient: If we moved? Well… I’ve thought about that, actually. Part of me thinks it might be quieter, because then I wouldn’t have to look out and wonder which neighbor is talking to who. But the kitchen would still be the kitchen, wouldn’t it? Nearly two years of this, and it’s not just a bad cabinet or a crooked drawer—though one drawer does stick, and my sister had the same problem in her laundry room remodel.\n\nWhat I mean is, I’m here because I’m sure the contractor and some people on this block have been working together to cheat us, or at least keep things wrong on purpose. So if we moved, maybe the neighbors wouldn’t be right there anymore, but I don’t know that it would undo what they’ve already done. People can still talk. Contractors know other contractors. It doesn’t just disappear because you pack boxes.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
If everyone said that? Including my husband? Well… my husband already says things like that. He’ll show me receipts and emails and say, “See, it’s just delays,” or “That’s what they charged everyone,” but receipts don’t explain why the kitchen is still half-wrong after nearly two years.

So no, I don’t think it would just change because they all denied it. People can deny anything. The contractor has excuses, the neighbors act like they don’t know what I’m talking about, and then somehow things still don’t get fixed right. It’s like when my cousin had trouble with her roof and everyone told her she was overreacting until the leak came through the ceiling—people always say you’re mistaken until it’s obvious.

I’m here because I’m sure the contractor and some people on my block have been quietly working together to cheat us, or keep it wrong on purpose. My husband means well, but even if he says they’re not, that doesn’t make me feel they aren’t.

---------- turn 16 | doctor:inference | doctor.build_inference_prompt() | model=meta-llama/Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct ----------
========== INPUT [doctor] ==========
[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… it’s the kitchen, mostly. Or that’s how it started, anyway. We had this contractor come in, and for almost two years now I’ve been sure he and some people on my block have been working together, quietly, to cheat us. They keep leaving things half-wrong on purpose, and then they act like I’m the one making a fuss.\n\nMy husband keeps showing me receipts and saying there are explanations, but receipts don’t tell you what people are really doing, do they? I mean, I’ve lived on that block a long time, I know when people are being funny. It’s been wearing me down.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people can't hear, related to these concerns about your neighbors and the contractor?\n\nPatient: Voices? No, I don’t think so. I mean, I hear plenty—cars, people out front, the usual banging around, especially when everyone’s trash cans are out, and Mrs. Hanley’s dog, honestly, that dog has been barking since it was a puppy—but I don’t think you mean that.\n\nI haven’t noticed, like… a voice talking to me that nobody else hears. It’s more what people do, the way things are left, the timing of it. That’s what I pay attention to.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people, the contractor and your neighbors, are specifically targeting you with their actions?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I mean, not like they announce it, of course, nobody’s going to be that foolish. But it feels very clear to me that the contractor and some people on the block have been working together, quietly, to cheat us and keep that kitchen half-wrong on purpose.\n\nAnd my husband says, “Look, here are the receipts,” and I know he thinks paperwork settles everything, but it doesn’t. Receipts don’t show the way someone pauses when you ask a question, or how suddenly everyone has an excuse. It’s been nearly two years of this. I wasn’t born yesterday.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that things you see or hear on TV, or read in the newspaper, are specifically about you or sending you a message?\n\nPatient: TV? No, not really TV. I don’t sit there thinking the news anchor is talking to me, if that’s what you mean.\n\nIt’s more right here, on my block, with the contractor and the kitchen. Nearly two years of it now—little things left wrong on purpose, people acting like they don’t know what I’m talking about, excuses every time. My husband keeps waving receipts around like that explains everything, but receipts don’t show what people are really up to. So no, not newspapers sending messages… it’s what’s happening in my own house.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't think the contractor and your neighbors were working against you?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, at first I tried to give everyone the benefit of the doubt—because mistakes happen, don’t they? My brother-in-law once had a bathroom done and the tile was crooked, and they blamed the supplier for weeks, so I know how these jobs can go.\n\nBut this has been nearly two years now. After a while you see the pattern. The contractor, and some people on the block too, they’ve been quietly working together to cheat us and leave my kitchen half-wrong on purpose. My husband keeps showing me receipts like that proves something, but receipts don’t explain all the excuses and the way things keep happening. So no, I can’t say there’s really been a time I didn’t think that, not once it became clear.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that you might be mistaken about the contractor and your neighbors working against you?\n\nPatient: Mistaken? I mean… people always say “maybe you’re mistaken” when they don’t want to look too closely. I’ve thought about that, of course. I’m not unreasonable.\n\nBut nearly two years is a long time to be “mistaken,” isn’t it? The kitchen still isn’t right, and every time there’s a reason, some excuse, and then my husband pulls out receipts like we’re in a courtroom. My cousin kept every receipt from her roof repair and still got taken advantage of, so paperwork doesn’t mean people are honest.\n\nSo no, I don’t really think I’m mistaken. I’m here because I’m sure the contractor and some people on my block have been working together quietly to cheat us and keep that kitchen half-wrong on purpose.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed most of the time since you started noticing these issues with the contractor and your neighbors?\n\nPatient: Sad? I don’t know if I’d call it that. I mean, anyone would be upset when their kitchen’s been left like that and people keep acting like it’s nothing. It wears on you.\n\nBut depressed most of the time… I’m not sure. I don’t sit around using those words. My sister used to say she was “down” whenever the weather changed, you know, especially in winter, and then she’d be fine once she got busy with something. So I don’t know exactly what you mean by depressed. I’m bothered, yes. Frustrated. But sad most of the time? I haven’t really thought of it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless about your situation with the contractor and your neighbors?\n\nPatient: Hopeless? I’m not sure that’s the word I’d use. I mean, some days it feels like talking to a brick wall, and that can wear a person down. But “hopeless” sounds so final, doesn’t it?\n\nMy mother used to say, if a drawer sticks long enough you stop noticing it until someone else opens it and says, “How do you live like this?” That’s sort of how the kitchen feels now—like everyone expects me to just get used to it. So I don’t know. I’m frustrated, I know that much. Hopeless… I’d have to think about what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about the contractor and your neighbors working against you ever changed or wavered over the nearly two years this has been going on?\n\nPatient: Changed? No, not really. I mean, there are days I get tired of talking about it, because then my husband starts with the receipts again, and I know receipts can make things look proper. My cousin had that happen with her roof—everything looked official until it didn’t.\n\nBut my mind hasn’t really wavered. For nearly two years now I’ve been sure the contractor and some people on my block have been quietly working together to cheat us and keep my kitchen half-wrong on purpose. The excuses change, the paperwork changes, but the feeling of what they’re doing hasn’t changed for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious most of the time since you started noticing these issues with the contractor and your neighbors?\n\nPatient: Anxious most of the time… I don’t know. I mean, I get keyed up when I think about it, sure, because who wouldn’t? You hire someone, you expect the work to be done right, and then suddenly everyone’s acting like you’re the unreasonable one.\n\nBut “most of the time” is hard to say. My aunt used to say she had nerves, you know, she’d wring a dish towel until it was practically twisted in half when the phone rang. I’m not like that exactly. I don’t sit there naming it anxiety. I’m alert, maybe. On edge when something reminds me of it. But I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling angry most of the time since you started noticing these issues with the contractor and your neighbors?\n\nPatient: Angry most of the time… I don’t know if I’d put it that way. I get irritated, yes, especially when I look at the kitchen and remember what was promised. But anger is a big word.\n\nMy father used to get angry—slam cabinets, bang tools around in the garage, that sort of thing. I’m not like that. I might mutter to myself or tell my husband he’s not seeing it clearly, but then I’ll go fold laundry or make tea and try to move on. Though, you know, even making tea in that kitchen reminds me of the whole thing, so it circles back.\n\nSo… angry most of the time? I’m not sure. Frustrated, definitely. Put out. But I’d have to think about whether that’s anger or just being tired of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could happen, or any information you could receive, that would change your mind about the contractor and your neighbors working against you?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think so, no. I mean, my husband keeps showing me receipts and messages and saying, “See, it’s all accounted for,” but receipts don’t tell you what people are saying behind your back, do they?\n\nNearly two years this has been going on, and I’m sure the contractor and some of the neighbors have been quietly working together to cheat us, or at least keep the kitchen wrong on purpose. They always have some explanation, some delay, some little excuse. My cousin had a plumber once who kept “forgetting” parts, and later it turned out—well, never mind, that’s a whole other story.\n\nSo unless someone actually admitted what they were doing, I don’t see what information would change it. Paperwork doesn’t prove people aren’t in on something.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities you normally enjoy since this issue with the contractor and your neighbors started?\n\nPatient: Lost interest? No, I don’t think so. I mean, the kitchen thing gets in the way mentally sometimes, because it’s right there in the house, and you can’t exactly avoid your own kitchen.\n\nBut I still go to church gatherings. I still go to book club, even if half the time someone brings up renovations and then I have to bite my tongue because people think they know better. And I keep up with the house, laundry, meals, seeing family, making plans the way I usually do. My daughter likes when we all get together on Sundays, and I still do that.\n\nSo no, I wouldn’t say I’ve stopped enjoying things. It’s more that this remodel business is sitting off to the side of everything, like a radio left on in another room.\n\nDoctor: Have you had difficulty interacting with friends or family because of your concerns about the contractor and your neighbors?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose it has made some things harder. I try not to bring it up all the time, because my daughter gets that look, you know, like she’s waiting for me to change the subject.\n\nWith the neighbors, I don’t trust certain ones anymore. I used to wave and chat about their gardens or who was putting out Christmas lights too early—there’s one house that starts in October, which I think is ridiculous—but now I keep it shorter. I don’t know who’s friendly and who’s just listening.\n\nAnd with workers… my husband has had to handle more of the remodeling decisions lately, because I keep questioning what they’re really doing and whether they’re being straight with us. He says it’s easier if he talks to them, but of course that makes me feel pushed aside a bit. Still, yes, it’s affected things.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the contractor and your neighbors would still be working against you if you moved to a different house?\n\nPatient: If we moved? Well… I’ve thought about that, actually. Part of me thinks it might be quieter, because then I wouldn’t have to look out and wonder which neighbor is talking to who. But the kitchen would still be the kitchen, wouldn’t it? Nearly two years of this, and it’s not just a bad cabinet or a crooked drawer—though one drawer does stick, and my sister had the same problem in her laundry room remodel.\n\nWhat I mean is, I’m here because I’m sure the contractor and some people on this block have been working together to cheat us, or at least keep things wrong on purpose. So if we moved, maybe the neighbors wouldn’t be right there anymore, but I don’t know that it would undo what they’ve already done. People can still talk. Contractors know other contractors. It doesn’t just disappear because you pack boxes.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about the contractor and neighbors would change if everyone involved, including your husband, told you they were not working against you?\n\nPatient: If everyone said that? Including my husband? Well… my husband already says things like that. He’ll show me receipts and emails and say, “See, it’s just delays,” or “That’s what they charged everyone,” but receipts don’t explain why the kitchen is still half-wrong after nearly two years.\n\nSo no, I don’t think it would just change because they all denied it. People can deny anything. The contractor has excuses, the neighbors act like they don’t know what I’m talking about, and then somehow things still don’t get fixed right. It’s like when my cousin had trouble with her roof and everyone told her she was overreacting until the leak came through the ceiling—people always say you’re mistaken until it’s obvious.\n\nI’m here because I’m sure the contractor and some people on my block have been quietly working together to cheat us, or keep it wrong on purpose. My husband means well, but even if he says they’re not, that doesn’t make me feel they aren’t.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

I don’t know… sad or empty most of the time for two weeks? I wouldn’t put it that way, exactly. I get upset, sure, especially when things in the house feel wrong or people act like I’m making too much of it, but sad all day, every day… I’m not sure.
